NOTE 8 - FAIR VALUE INFORMATION

Accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America establish a framework for
measuring fair value. That framework provides a fair value hierarchy that prioritizes the inputs to
valuation techniques used to measure fair value. The hierarchy gives the highest priority to unadjusted
quoted prices in active markets for identical assets or liabilities (Level 1 measurements) and the lowest

priority to unobservable inputs (Level 3 measurements). The three levels of the fair value hierarchy are
described below:

Level 1

Inputs to the valuation methodology are unadjusted quoted prices for identical assets or liabilities in
active markets that the Company has the ability to access.

Level 2
Inputs to the valuation methodology include:

e¢ Quoted prices for similar assets or liabilities in active markets;

e Quoted prices for identical or similar assets or liabilities in inactive markets;
e Inputs other than quoted prices that are observable for the asset or liability;
e

Inputs that are derived principally from or corroborated by observable market data by correlation
or other means.

If the asset or liability has a specified (contractual) term, the Level 2 input must be observable for
substantially the full term of the asset or liability.

Level 3
Inputs to the valuation methodology are unobservable and significant to the fair value measurement.

The asset's or liability’s fair value measurement level within the fair value hierarchy is based on the
lowest level of any input that is significant to the fair value measurement. Valuation techniques used
need to maximize the use of observable inputs and minimize the use of unobservable inputs.

The following is a description of the valuation methodologies used for financial instruments measured
at fair value:

Mutual Funds

Mutual funds are valued using Level 1 inputs, which consist of the quoted price of shares held by the
Company at year end.

There have been no changes in the methodologies used at December 31, 2017 and 2016. See Note 4
for additional information regarding investments.

The methods described above may produce a fair value calculation that may not be indicative of net
realizable value or reflective of future fair values. Furthermore, while the Company believes its
valuation methods are appropriate and consistent with other market participants, the use of different
methodologies or assumptions to determine the fair value of certain financial instruments could result in
a different fair value measurement at the reporting date.

There were no transfers between levels of investments during the years ended December 31, 2017 and
2016.

NOTE 9 - RELATED PARTY TRANSACTIONS
Consulting Agreement

The Company entered into a consulting agreement with related parties on April 1, 2013. The
agreement expires no later than March 31, 2033 and calls for monthly payments of $11,667 plus certain
expenses. Commencing August 1, 2015, both parties agreed to reduce the monthly payments to
$5,833 plus certain expenses. The agreement was terminated effective October 8, 2017, and there are
no outstanding commitments as of December 31, 2017. The Company incurred and paid consulting
fees of $65,860 and $79,032 for operational support for the years ended December 31, 2017 and 2016,
respectively.

Operating Income from Owner

The Company provided healthcare services to a party related to the owner during the year ended
December 31, 2016 in the amount of $72,720 which is included in operating income. As a result, a
noncash distribution to the owner in the amount of $72,720 was made during the year December 31,
2016, which has been excluded from financing activities on the consolidated statements of cash flows.
NOTE 10 - PENSION PLAN

The Company has a 401(k) pension plan. Beginning in January 1, 2014, the Company began making
discretionary matching contributions on up to 2% of participants’ eligible compensation. The Company,
at its discretion, may also make a profit-sharing contribution. Matching and profit-sharing contribution
expense charged to operations totaled $28,744 and $31,308 for the years ended December 31, 2017
and 2016, respectively.

NOTE 11 - CONCENTRATIONS OF CREDIT RISK

Cash Deposits

The Company maintains cash balances at a federally insured financial institution. The Company’s
credit risk with respect to such balances is all amounts on deposit in excess of federally insured limits.

Accounts Receivable

NOTE 12 - HEALTHCARE INDUSTRY

The healthcare industry is subject to numerous laws and regulations of federal, state and local
governments. These laws and regulations include, but are not necessarily limited to, matters such as
licensure, accreditation, government healthcare program participation requirements, reimbursement for
patient services, and Medicare fraud and abuse. Government activity has increased with respect to
investigations and allegations concerning possible violations of fraud and abuse statutes and
regulations by healthcare providers. Violations of these laws and regulations could result in expulsion
from government healthcare programs together with the imposition of significant fines and penalties, as
well as significant repayments for patient services previously billed. Management believes that the
Company is in compliance with fraud and abuse regulations as well as other applicable government
laws and regulations. While no material regulatory inquiries have been made, compliance with such
laws and regulations can be subject to future government review and interpretation, as well as
regulatory actions unknown or unasserted at this time.

NOTE 13 - CONSOLIDATION OF VARIABLE INTEREST ENTITIES

KAR leases its facility from Realty; the companies are under common control. Following accounting
principles generally accepted in the United States of America for variable interest entities, KAR is the
primary beneficiary of Realty because KAR has the power to direct the activities of Realty that most
significantly impact Realty’s economic performance. Those activities include maintenance of the
property and joint liability with respect to the long-term debt secured by the property.

Should additional financial support be needed for Realty in the future, it is expected that KAR will
provide that additional financial support due to the ownership and leasing relationship between KAR
and Realty. The current sole source of Realty’s revenue is the lease payments made by KAR. Realty
creditors have recourse to the assets of KAR due to the companies’ joint liability on the long-term debt
relating to the land and building.

The assets of Realty are collateral for the related long-term debt and are restricted to settle long-term
debt obligations of Realty, should Realty default on its monthly principal and interest payments.

We have reviewed the accompanying consolidated financial statements of KAR Little Box,
LLC d/b/a Silver Creek Manor (the Company), which comprise the consolidated balance
sheets as of December 31, 2018 and 2017, and the related consolidated statements of
income, owner's equity and cash flows for the years then ended, and the related notes to
the consolidated financial statements. A review includes primarily applying analytical
procedures to management's financial data and making inquiries of Company
management. A review is substantially less in scope than an audit, the objective of which is
the expression of an opinion regarding the consolidated financial statements as a whole.
Accordingly, we do not express such an opinion.

Management’s Responsibility for the Consolidated Financial Statements

Management is responsible for the preparation and fair presentation of the consolidated
financial statements in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the
United States of America; this includes the design, implementation and maintenance of
internal control relevant to the preparation and fair presentation of the consolidated financial
statements that are free from material misstatement whether due to fraud or error.

Accountants’ Responsibility

Our responsibility is to conduct the review engagements in accordance with Statements on
Standards for Accounting and Review Services promulgated by the Accounting and Review
Services Committee of the AICPA. Those standards require us to perform procedures to
obtain limited assurance as a basis for reporting whether we are aware of any material
modifications that should be made to the consolidated financial statements for them to be in
accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America.

We believe that the results of our procedures provide a reasonable basis for our
conclusion.

Accountants’ Conclusion
Based on our reviews, we are not aware of any material modifications that should be made

to the accompanying consolidated financial statements in order for them to be in
accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America.

NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS

NOTE 1 - NATURE OF BUSINESS

KAR Little Box, LLC d/b/a Silver Creek Manor (KAR) is a skilled nursing facility operating in Bristol,
Rhode Island, and participates in the Medicare and Medicaid programs. The facility is licensed by the
Rhode Island Department of Health for a maximum capacity of 128 beds and is currently operating as a
118-bed facility.

Silver Creek Realty, Inc. (Realty) leases the real estate to KAR. The companies are related through
common ownership. KAR and Realty are herein referred to as the Company. In addition, Realty
generates rental income from leasing a residential property to an unrelated third party.

NOTE 2 - SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES

Principles of Consolidation

The accompanying consolidated financial statements include the accounts of KAR and Realty. The
companies are under common control. All significant intercompany transactions and balances have
been eliminated in the consolidated financial statements.

Use of Estimates

The preparation of consolidated financial statements in conformity with accounting principles generally
accepted in the United States of America requires management to make estimates and assumptions
that affect the reported amounts of assets and liabilities and disclosure of contingent assets and
liabilities at the date of the consolidated financial statements. Estimates also affect the reported

amounts of revenues and expenses during the reporting period. Actual results could differ from those
estimates.

Cash and Cash Equivalents

The Company considers all highly liquid investments with original maturities of three months or less to
be cash equivalents.

Accounts Receivable

Accounts receivable are carried at cost, less an allowance for doubtful accounts of $61,502 at
December 31, 2018 and 2017. On a periodic basis, the Company evaluates its accounts receivable
and adjusts the allowance for doubtful accounts, based on a history of past write-offs and collections
and current credit conditions. Accounts are written off based on management’s evaluation of the
collectability of each account resulting from collection efforts.

Investments

Investments in equity securities with readily determinable fair values and all investments in debt
securities are reported at fair value in the consolidated balance sheets. Fair value is the price that
would be received to sell an asset or paid to transfer a liability in an orderly transaction between market
participants at the measurement date. See Note 8 for a discussion of fair value measurements.

7

===== PAGE 279 =====
[OCR]
KAR LITTLE BOX, LLC
D/B/A SILVER CREEK MANOR

NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
See Independent Accountants’ Review Report

Property, Equipment and Depreciation

Property and equipment are recorded at cost. Depreciation is calculated on the straight-line and
accelerated methods over the expected useful lives of the assets.

Impairment of Long-Lived Assets

The Company reviews long-lived assets for impairment whenever events or changes in circumstances
indicate that the carrying amount of an asset may not be recoverable. Recoverability of assets to be
held and used is measured by a comparison of the carrying amount of an asset to estimated future
cash flows expected to be generated by the asset. [If the carrying amount of an asset exceeds its
estimated future cash flows, an impairment charge is recognized for the amount by which the carrying
amount of the asset exceeds the fair value of the asset.

Operating Income

Operating income is reported at the estimated net realizable amounts from patients, third-party payors
and others for services rendered. Rates charged for services rendered, other than private pay
residents, are regulated by Medicaid, Medicare and other third-party programs. Revenue from private
pay residents is based on prevailing rates.

Approximately 63% and 68% of the Company’s operating income for the years ended December 31,
2018 and 2017, respectively, were funded by Medicare, Medicaid and other State assistance programs.

Revenue under third-party payor agreements is subject to audit and retroactive adjustment. Provisions
for estimated third-party settlements are provided in the period the related services are rendered.
Differences between the estimated amounts accrued and interim or final settlements are reported in
operations in the year of settlement. Management believes that all applicable reimbursement principles
have been properly applied, and any settlement amounts will not have a material impact on the
Company’s financial position or results of operations.

Income Taxes

KAR and Realty elected to be taxed as “S” corporations under the provisions of the Internal Revenue
Code. Under these provisions, the companies are not required to pay federal or state corporate
income taxes on their taxable income. Instead, the owner is liable for individual federal and state
income taxes on the companies’ taxable income.

Accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America require management to
evaluate tax positions taken by the Company and recognize a tax liability if the Company has taken an
uncertain position that more likely than not would not be sustained upon examination by the Internal
Revenue Service. The Company is subject to routine audits by taxing jurisdictions; however, there are
currently no audits for any tax periods in progress. The Company recognizes interest and penalties, if
applicable, as a component of administrative and general expenses.

Subsequent Events

Subsequent events have been evaluated through March 13, 2019, the date the financial statements are
available to be issued.

NOTE 5 - DUE TO THIRD-PARTY PAYORS

of overpayments from the Medicaid program.

NOTE 6 - LINE OF CREDIT

Journal prime rate. The line of credit is collateralized by all assets and property held by KAR and is
guaranteed by Realty. The amount available on the line of credit at December 31, 2018 and 2017 was
$300,000.

NOTE 8 - FAIR VALUE INFORMATION

Accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America establish a framework for
measuring fair value. That framework provides a fair value hierarchy that prioritizes the inputs to
valuation techniques used to measure fair value. The hierarchy gives the highest priority to unadjusted
quoted prices in active markets for identical assets or liabilities (Level 1 measurements) and the lowest

priority to unobservable inputs (Level 3 measurements). The three levels of the fair value hierarchy are
described below:

Level 1

Inputs to the valuation methodology are unadjusted quoted prices for identical assets or liabilities in
active markets that the Company has the ability to access.

Level 2
Inputs to the valuation methodology include:

e Quoted prices for similar assets or liabilities in active markets;

e Quoted prices for identical or similar assets or liabilities in inactive markets;
e Inputs other than quoted prices that are observable for the asset or liability;
e

Inputs that are derived principally from or corroborated by observable market data by correlation
or other means.

If the asset or liability has a specified (contractual) term, the Level 2 input must be observable for
substantially the full term of the asset or liability.

Level 3
Inputs to the valuation methodology are unobservable and significant to the fair value measurement.

===== PAGE 283 =====

D/B/A SILVER CREEK MANOR

NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
See Independent Accountants’ Review Report

The asset's or liability’s fair value measurement level within the fair value hierarchy is based on the
lowest level of any input that is significant to the fair value measurement. Valuation techniques used
need to maximize the use of observable inputs and minimize the use of unobservable inputs.

The following is a description of the valuation methodologies used for financial instruments measured
at fair value:

Mutual Funds
Mutual funds are valued using Level 1 inputs, which consist of the quoted price of shares held by the
Company at year end.

There have been no changes in the methodologies used at December 31, 2018 and 2017. See Note 4
for additional information regarding investments.

The methods described above may produce a fair value calculation that may not be indicative of net
realizable value or reflective of future fair values. Furthermore, while the Company believes its
valuation methods are appropriate and consistent with other market participants, the use of different
methodologies or assumptions to determine the fair value of certain financial instruments could result in
a different fair value measurement at the reporting date.

There were no transfers between levels of investments during the years ended December 31, 2018 and
2017.

NOTE 9 - RELATED PARTY TRANSACTIONS
Consulting Agreement

The Company entered into a consulting agreement with related parties that called for monthly
payments of $5,833 plus certain expenses. The agreement was terminated effective October 8, 2017,
and there are no outstanding commitments as of December 31, 2018. The Company incurred and paid
consulting fees of $-0- and $65,860 for operational support for the years ended December 31, 2018
and 2017, respectively.

NOTE 10 - PENSION PLAN

The Company sponsors a 401(k) pension plan. In accordance with the plan, the Company makes
discretionary matching contributions on up to 2% of participants’ eligible compensation. The Company,
at its discretion, may also make a profit-sharing contribution. Matching and profit-sharing contribution
expense charged to operations totaled $27,605 and $28,744 for the years ended December 31, 2018
and 2017, respectively.

NOTE 12 - HEALTHCARE INDUSTRY

The healthcare industry is subject to numerous laws and regulations of federal, state and local
governments. These laws and regulations include, but are not necessarily limited to, matters such as
licensure, accreditation, government healthcare program participation requirements, reimbursement for
patient services, and Medicare fraud and abuse. Government activity has increased with respect to
investigations and allegations concerning possible violations of fraud and abuse statutes and
regulations by healthcare providers. Violations of these laws and regulations could result in expulsion
from government healthcare programs together with the imposition of significant fines and penalties, as
well as significant repayments for patient services previously billed. Management believes that the
Company is in compliance with fraud and abuse regulations as well as other applicable government
laws and regulations. While no material regulatory inquiries have been made, compliance with such
laws and regulations can be subject to future government review and interpretation, as well as
regulatory actions unknown or unasserted at this time.

NOTE 13 - CONSOLIDATION OF VARIABLE INTEREST ENTITIES

KAR leases its facility from Realty; the companies are under common control. Following accounting
principles generally accepted in the United States of America for variable interest entities, KAR is the
primary beneficiary of Realty because KAR has the power to direct the activities of Realty that most
significantly impact Realty’s economic performance. Those activities include maintenance of the
property and joint liability with respect to the long-term debt secured by the property.

Should additional financial support be needed for Realty in the future, it is expected that KAR will
provide that additional financial support due to the ownership and leasing relationship between KAR
and Realty. The current sole source of Realty’s revenue is the lease payments made by KAR. Realty

creditors have recourse to the assets of KAR due to the companies’ joint liability on the long-term debt
relating to the land and building.

The assets of Realty are collateral for the related long-term debt and are restricted to settle long-term
debt obligations of Realty, should Realty default on its monthly principal and interest payments.

We have reviewed the accompanying consolidated financial statements of KAR Little Box,
LLC d/b/a Silver Creek Manor (the Company), which comprise the consolidated balance
sheets as of December 31, 2019 and 2018, and the related consolidated statements of
income, owner's equity and cash flows for the years then ended, and the related notes to
the consolidated financial statements. A review includes primarily applying analytical
procedures to management's financial data and making inquiries of Company management.
A review is substantially less in scope than an audit, the objective of which is the expression
of an opinion regarding the consolidated financial statements as a whole. Accordingly, we do
not express such an opinion.

Management’s Responsibility for the Financial Statements

Management is responsible for the preparation and fair presentation of the consolidated
financial statements in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the
United States of America; this includes the design, implementation and maintenance of
internal contro! relevant to the preparation and fair presentation of the consolidated financial
statements that are free from material misstatement whether due to fraud or error.

Accountants’ Responsibility

Our responsibility is to conduct the review engagements in accordance with Statements on
Standards for Accounting and Review Services promulgated by the Accounting and Review
Services Committee of the AICPA. Those standards require us to perform procedures to
obtain limited assurance as a basis for reporting whether we are aware of any material
modifications that should be made to the consolidated financial statements for them to be in
accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America.
We believe that the results of our procedures provide a reasonable basis for our conclusion.

Accountants’ Conclusion

Based on our reviews, we are not aware of any material modifications that should be made
to the accompanying consolidated financial statements in order for them to be in accordance
with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America.

conclusion is not modified with respect to this matter.

NOTE 1 - NATURE OF BUSINESS

KAR Little Box, LLC d/b/a Silver Creek Manor (KAR) is a skilled nursing facility operating in Bristol,
Rhode Island, and participates in the Medicare and Medicaid programs. The facility is licensed by the
Rhode Island Department of Health for a maximum capacity of 128 beds and is currently operating as a
118-bed facility.

Silver Creek Realty, Inc. (Realty) leases the real estate to KAR. The companies are related through
common ownership. KAR and Realty are herein referred to as the Company. In addition, Realty
generates rental income from leasing a residential property to an unrelated third party.

NOTE 2 - SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES

Principles of Consolidation

The accompanying consolidated financial statements include the accounts of KAR and Realty. The
companies are under common control. All significant intercompany transactions and balances have
been eliminated in the consolidated financial statements.

Change in Accounting Principle

In May 2014, the Financial Accounting Standards Board issued Accounting Standards Update (ASU)
No. 2014-09, Revenue from Contracts with Customers (Topic 606), which replaces numerous
requirements in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of
America, including industry-specific requirements, and provides companies with a single revenue
recognition model for recognizing revenue from contracts with customers. The core principle of the
new standard is that a company should recognize revenue to depict the transfer of promised goods or
services to customers in an amount that reflects the consideration to which the company expects to be
entitled in exchange for those goods or services. The two permitted transition methods under the new
standard are the full retrospective method, in which case the standard would be applied to each prior
reporting period presented and the cumulative effect of applying the standard would be recognized at
the earliest period shown, or the modified retrospective method, in which case the cumulative effect of
applying the standard would be recognized at the date of initial application. The new standard is
effective for annual reporting periods beginning after December 15, 2018. Management has adopted
ASU 2014-09 for the year ended December 31, 2019. The amendments have been applied using the
modified retrospective method.

There was no cumulative effect of applying ASU 2014-09.

Use of Estimates

The preparation of consolidated financial statements in conformity with accounting principles generally
accepted in the United States of America requires management to make estimates and assumptions
that affect the reported amounts of assets and liabilities and disclosure of contingent assets and
liabilities at the date of the consolidated financial statements. Estimates also affect the reported

amounts of revenues and expenses during the reporting period. Actual results could differ from those
estimates.

===== PAGE 297 =====
[OCR]
KAR LITTLE BOX, LLC
D/B/A SILVER CREEK MANOR

NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
See Independent Accountants’ Review Report

Cash and Cash Equivalents

The Company considers all highly liquid investments with original maturities of three months or less to
be cash equivalents.

Accounts Receivable

Accounts receivable are carried at cost, less an allowance for doubtful accounts of $100,000 and
$61,502 at December 31, 2019 and 2018, respectively. On a periodic basis, the Company evaluates its
accounts receivable and adjusts the allowance for doubtful accounts, based on a history of past write-
offs and collections and current credit conditions. Accounts are written off based on management's
evaluation of the collectability of each account resulting from collection efforts.

Investments

Investments in equity securities with readily determinable fair values and all investments in debt
securities are reported at fair value in the consolidated balance sheets. Fair value is the price that
would be received to sell an asset or paid to transfer a liability in an orderly transaction between market
participants at the measurement date. See Note 8 for a discussion of fair value measurements.

Property, Equipment and Depreciation

Property and equipment are recorded at cost. Depreciation is calculated on the straight-line and
accelerated methods over the expected useful lives of the assets.

Impairment of Long-Lived Assets

The Company reviews long-lived assets for impairment whenever events or changes in circumstances
indicate that the carrying amount of an asset may not be recoverable. Recoverability of assets to be
held and used is measured by a comparison of the carrying amount of an asset to estimated future
cash flows expected to be generated by the asset. If the carrying amount of an asset exceeds its
estimated future cash flows, an impairment charge is recognized for the amount by which the carrying
amount of the asset exceeds the fair value of the asset.

Daily Service Revenue

Daily service revenue is reported at the amount that reflects the consideration the Company expects to
receive, the estimated transaction price, in exchange for providing resident care. These amounts are
due from residents (private pay), third-party payors (including health insurers and government payors)
and others. Performance obligations are determined based on the nature of the services provided.
Daily service revenue is recognized as performance obligations are satisfied. The Company
recognizes revenue on a per diem basis in accordance with the provisions of ASC 606, Revenue from
Contracts with Customers. Rates charged for services rendered, other than private-pay residents, are
regulated by Medicaid, Medicare and other third-party programs. Revenue from private-pay residents
is based on prevailing rates. Approximately 64% and 63% of the Company’s net patient service
revenue for the years ended 2019 and 2018, respectively, were funded by the Medicare, Medicaid and
other state assistance programs.

===== PAGE 298 =====
[OCR]
KAR LITTLE BOX, LLC
D/B/A SILVER CREEK MANOR

NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
See Independent Accountants’ Review Report

Settlements with third-party payors for retroactive adjustments resulting from audits, reviews or
investigations are included in the determination of the estimated transaction price for providing
services. The Company estimates the transaction price based on the terms of the contract with the
payor, correspondence with the payor and historical payment trends, and retroactive adjustments are
recognized in future periods as final settlements are determined.

Laws and regulations concerning government programs, including Medicare and Medicaid, are complex
and subject to varying interpretation. These laws and regulations include, but are not necessarily
limited to, matters such as licensure, accreditation, government health care program participation
requirements (which are a precondition to the receipt of reimbursement for patient services), the
Medicare False Claims Act, the Stark Anti-Referral Act, the Anti-Kickback legislation, and other
Medicaid fraud and abuse legislation. Government activity has increased with respect to investigations
that have ted to allegations concerning possible violations by health care providers of those statutes
and regulations. Violations of these laws and regulations could result in expulsion from government
health care programs, together with the imposition of significant fines and penalties, as well as
significant repayments for patient services previously billed and received. Management believes that
the Company is in compliance with fraud and abuse regulations as well as other applicable government
laws and regulations. While no material regulatory inquiries have been made during 2019, compliance
with such laws and regulations can be subject to future government review and interpretation, as well
as regulatory actions unknown or unasserted at this time.

The Company has elected the practical expedient under ASC 606 and does not adjust the promised
amount of consideration from patients and third-party payors for the effects of a significant financing
component due to the Company’s expectation that the period between the time the service is provided
to a patient and the time that the patient or a third-party payor pays for that service will be one year or
less. However, the Company does, in certain instances, enter into payment agreements with residents
that allow payments in excess of one year. For those cases, the financing component is not deemed to
be significant to the contract.

Income Taxes

KAR and Realty elected to be taxed as “S” corporations under the provisions of the Internal Revenue
Code. Under those provisions, the Company is not liable for federal corporate income taxes. Instead,
the owner is liable for individual federal income taxes on their respective shares of the Company’s
taxable income. Accordingly, the consolidated financial statements reflect no provision or liability for
federal income taxes. Similarly, the 2018 consolidated financial statements reflect no provision or
liability for state income taxes since the amounts were reported by the individual stockholders.

On July 5, 2019, the State of Rhode Island enacted House Bill 5151 which established a new tax on
pass-through entities operating in the state. The new tax is effective for tax years beginning on or after

January 1, 2019. The Company incurred and paid state income taxes of $54,200 the year ended
December 31, 2019.

Accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America require management to
evaluate tax positions taken by the Company and recognize a tax liability if the Company has taken an
uncertain position that more likely than not would not be sustained upon examination by the Internal
Revenue Service. The Company is subject to routine audits by taxing jurisdictions; however, there are
currently no audits for any tax periods in progress. The Company recognizes interest and penalties, if
applicable, as a component of administrative and general expenses.

NOTE 8 - FAIR VALUE INFORMATION

Accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America establish a framework for
measuring fair value. That framework provides a fair value hierarchy that prioritizes the inputs to
valuation techniques used to measure fair value. The hierarchy gives the highest priority to unadjusted
quoted prices in active markets for identical assets or liabilities (Level 1 measurements) and the lowest
priority to unobservable inputs (Level 3 measurements). The three levels of the fair value hierarchy are
described below:

Level 1
Inputs to the valuation methodology are unadjusted quoted prices for identical assets or liabilities in
active markets that the Company has the ability to access.

Level 2
Inputs to the valuation methodology include:

Quoted prices for similar assets or liabilities in active markets;

Quoted prices for identical or similar assets or liabilities in inactive markets;

Inputs other than quoted prices that are observable for the asset or liability;

Inputs that are derived principally from or corroborated by observable market data by correlation
or other means.

If the asset or liability has a specified (contractual) term, the Level 2 input must be observable for
substantially the full term of the asset or liability.

Level 3
Inputs to the valuation methodology are unobservable and significant to the fair value measurement.

-12-

===== PAGE 302 =====
[OCR]
KAR LITTLE BOX, LLC
D/B/A SILVER CREEK MANOR

NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
See Independent Accountants’ Review Report

The asset's or liability’s fair value measurement level within the fair value hierarchy is based on the
lowest level of any input that is significant to the fair value measurement. Valuation techniques used
need to maximize the use of observable inputs and minimize the use of unobservable inputs.

The following is a description of the valuation methodologies used for financial instruments measured at
fair value:

Mutual Funds

Mutual funds are valued using Level 1 inputs, which consist of the quoted price of shares held by the
Company at year end.

There have been no changes in the methodologies used at December 31, 2019 and 2018. See Note 4
for additional information regarding investments.

The methods described above may produce a fair value calculation that may not be indicative of net
realizable value or reflective of future fair values. Furthermore, while the Company believes its
valuation methods are appropriate and consistent with other market participants, the use of different
methodologies or assumptions to determine the fair value of certain financial instruments could result in
a different fair value measurement at the reporting date.

There were no transfers between levels of investments during the years ended December 31, 2019 and
2018.

NOTE 9 - PENSION PLAN

The Company sponsors a 401(k) pension plan. In accordance with the plan, the Company makes
discretionary matching contributions on up to 2% of participants’ eligible compensation. The Company,
at its discretion, may also make a profit-sharing contribution. Matching and profit-sharing contribution
expense charged to operations totaled $27,398 and $27,605 for the years ended December 31, 2019
and 2018, respectively.

NOTE 11 - CONSOLIDATION OF VARIABLE INTEREST ENTITIES

KAR leases its facility from Realty; the companies are under common control. Following accounting
principles generally accepted in the United States of America for variable interest entities, KAR is the
primary beneficiary of Realty because KAR has the power to direct the activities of Realty that most
significantly impact Realty’s economic performance. Those activities include maintenance of the
property and joint liability with respect to the long-term debt secured by the property.

Should additional financial support be needed for Realty in the future, it is expected that KAR will
provide that additional financial support due to the ownership and leasing relationship between KAR
and Realty. The current sole source of Realty’s revenue is the lease payments made by KAR. Realty
creditors have recourse to the assets of KAR due to the companies’ joint liability on the long-term debt
relating to the land and building.

The assets of Realty are collateral for the related long-term debt and are restricted to settle long-term
debt obligations of Realty, should Realty default on its monthly principal and interest payments.

To Whom It May Concern,

We are submitting this letter in connection with Silver Creek Manor SNF LLC, Berkshire Place SNF,
LLC, and GreenTree Nursing and Rehab LLC’s (collectively, the “Applicant”) applications for a Change
in Effect Control of Silver Creek Manor, Berkshire Place Nursing & Rehabilitation Center, and Saint
Elizabeth Manor East Bay (collectively, the “Applications”), respectively. The financial information
provided to you, compiled by the Applicant and attached to this application at Tab 28, accurately presents
the financial condition and results of operations of Preferred Care at Wall, Preferred Care at Mercer,
Preferred Care at Old Bridge, Preferred Care at Absecon, Preferred Care at Hamilton, Aria Nursing &
Rehab, Allegra Nursing & Rehab, Avista Nursing & Rehab, Carriage House Nursing & Rehab (purchased
November 1, 2020), Collanades Assisted Living (purchased November 1, 2020), Potomac Valley,
Solomons (purchased September 1, 2020), and Hermitage AL (purchased September 1, 2020) through
November 30, 2020. Unless specifically noted, the facilities were owned prior to 2020.

    1) Page 1 of the attachment presents the (i) bed licenses, (ii) revenue, (iii) operating expenses,
       EBITDAR, (iv) Cash on Hand at January 31, 2021, and (v) the Retained Profits in each of the
       Facilities through November 30, 2020.
    2) Page 2 shows the Management Income Net Profit and Net Cash Flow for the management
       companies which are under common control.

In evaluating the Applicant’s affiliated entities’ financial condition, the EBITDAR, and Cash flow, show
the Entities ability to earn income from the Facilities, and the retained profits show the significant
investment made in the facilities.

Additionally, the Applicants’ affiliates have been in the nursing home industry for many years and have
never experienced any failures or defaults. The Applicants’ affiliates also have the banking and industry
relationships needed to ensure success.

Based on the financial information provided herein, the history of the Applicants’ principals and
investors’ successful operation of skilled nursing facilities, the tax returns which we prepare on behalf of
the owners of the facilities, the certified audits we have prepared for other facilities owned and operated
by investors in the Applicants, we certify that in our professional opinion, the Applicants have the
financial ability to consummate the purchase of and to safely operate Silver Creek Manor, Berkshire Place
Nursing & Rehabilitation Center, and Saint Elizabeth Manor East Bay.

                                     LETTER AGREEMENT

       THIS LETTER AGREEMENT (“Agreement”), dated as of January 28, 2021 is among
SILVER CREEK MANOR SNF LLC, a Rhode Island limited liability company (“Operator”) and
BORUCH MERMELSTEIN, DOV GREEN, SIMON STERN AND AHARON STERN
(“Investors”).

                                        W I T N E S S E T H:

        A.     Operator has submitted its application to the Rhode Island of Health for approval
to assume the operations of the facility located at 7 Creek Lane and 3 Creek Lane in Bristol, Rhode
Island, which is commonly known as “Silver Creek Manor Nursing and Rehabilitation Center”
and improved by a duly licensed 128-bed skilled nursing facility (the “Facility”).

        B.     As a condition of receiving the consent of the Rhode Island Department of Health
for the Operator to assume said operations, the Rhode Island Department of Health has requested
that the Operator demonstrate financial stability and capital in an amount to operate the Facility,
absent revenue, at full capacity, for no less than 30 days.

       C.      Investors, who are members of the Operator’s entity, personally agree to fund the
required capital to meet the requirements of the Rhode Island Department of Health.

       NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the foregoing and the mutual covenants herein
contained, and for other good and valuable consideration, it is hereby agreed as follows:

      1.      Annexed hereto as Exhibit A are the proof of funds from the Investors that
demonstrate their financial ability to comply with the requirements of the Rhode Island
Department of Health.

        2.      Investors hereby state that they will fund the necessary amounts of cash to the
Facility, for any and all operational needs, in the event the Facility is required to fund, in full, the
day to day operations of the Facility for a minimum of thirty (30) days.

       3.      This Agreement shall be binding upon and inure to the benefit of the respective
successors and assigns of each of the parties hereto, but does not otherwise create, and shall not be
construed as creating, any rights enforceable by any person not a party to this Agreement.

        4.      This Agreement shall be governed as to validity, interpretations, enforcement and
effect by the laws of the State of Rhode Island without giving effect to conflicts of law principles
thereunder.

        5.      This Agreement may be signed by the parties hereto in several counterparts.
Delivery of a photocopy, facsimile or .pdf copy of an executed counterpart of this Agreement shall
be effective as delivery of a manually executed original counterpart of this Agreement.
